ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3848	In this area, there is the need – and this is the consequence of development – for a second is aimed at building in Vienna, for the following reasons: According to the law, each national court must have a detention room for prisoners of investigation, which is the so-called national prisons. We have an additional burden in Austria in the area of those in detention, but we cannot extend the Vienna-Josefstadt judicial centre for legal and factual reasons as far as the detention area is concerned. It would be possible to expand by a maximum of 100 beds, but we need 700 to 800 in Vienna, namely a prison room for prisoners. Therefore, as the only consequence of this need, there is only the possibility of establishing a second court in Vienna with a national court prison, because, logically, the investigators – and I repeat: only those who are concerned – must be accommodated where they are to be consulted. That means: The investigating prisoner will be arrested on Friday and will be heard on Saturday and Sunday. If he were housed in Mistelbach, which, of course, would be theoretically possible, this would be organisationally impossible and would violate the legal situation. That's why the judges' proposals – I suppose you mean them – are simply absurd, where the opinion is that we could save ourselves a second court building in Vienna – if the development persists! ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e4770	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e4785	This is particularly critical in this respect, because there is also a lack of justification for this so-called European Public Prosecutor's Office by the EU Commission. These are the following: Austria is a very well-functioning rule of law. The rule of law consists of two legs: One is the rule of law, and the other is its implementation by the state itself and not by other forces. Austria has still successfully and effectively combated the offences committed in Austria, regardless of whether they were national or international. A European Procuratorate now has the tendency, without clearly indicating the government structure, to give the Austrian prosecutors the right to set rules on what offences are and which are not. But these proposals cannot be controlled for us. For me, the European Public Prosecutor's Office is a loss of sovereignty that we do not need. Our constructive counter-proposal is that European bodies can participate in Austrian criminal proceedings as private parties with a view on file. Then they can make sure how well the system is running, and they can make additional requests for proof. But we don't want a super-super-state attorneyship about ours. But I do not argue that the trend is going.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e6468	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It is not always easy to be the animal protection spokesman of the ÖVP because the ÖVP has always tried to bring the very legitimate concerns of animal welfare, but also the very important concerns of the Austrian farms to the farm. [[Abg. Arbitrators: Right!]] And that's not always easy, ladies and gentlemen. As a result, I am all the more proud and happy that I am standing here today and we have made a four-party application in matters of a federal uniform animal welfare law. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I would also like to thank you at this point. First of all, I should like to thank the companies that have to live with it and which we must also provide assistance. Secondly, I would like to thank the animal protection spokespersons of the other political groups who have, over the past 16 months, enabled a genuinely subject-oriented, solution-oriented negotiation, which ultimately means that we have come to a very good and very suitable draft law, which we are presenting to you today for decision-making. But I also thank the experts – many of them in the number – who have worked here. At this point, I would like to stress three gentlemen in particular and mention them as vice-presidents for everyone else. These are Professor Troxler, Mag. Gsandtner and Director Pechlaner of the Tiergarten Schönbrunn, as well as all colleagues from the Ministries, who have always been available to us in many hours of night meetings, to which questions, wishes, complaints have been addressed and have always approached this question in a very factual and solution-oriented way. [[In the case of the ÖVP, members of the liberals and the Agg. May. Sima.]] It was a difficult way, and you also have to explain why this has been such a difficult way: Animal welfare. According to a survey, animal welfare is a very important concern for 45 percent of people, 43 percent of whom are concerned. That is, more than 80 percent of people take part in animal welfare. And what I noticed, even in the past years, when I've been working on it: Many people know about animal welfare – which is understandable, since there are also a lot of pet owners, many people who work with animals that live with animals and who naturally have a very own relationship with them and also want to recognize themselves and share their knowledge accordingly in this relationship. That is why we have known from the very beginning that we are going on a path in which we are trying to work with experts on a scientific basis of this Animal Protection Act. I think that in recent months – the discussions have shown it – this was probably the only way to really get together here. For us – I would like to expressly say this here – it has always been and always is important to find balance. For us, balance is a very essential point in these issues, namely the balance between animal welfare, the people who are emotionally unbelievably dependent on the way in which animals take a large part when they see animal husbandries which, in their opinion, are not animal-friendly, but also those people, whom we also need to give help, who are living by these animals who have to deal with them in the farms, have to deal with them daily, which are also of great concern to the animals, but who often have quite different access to them. These very different approaches are often very difficult to match. But, I think, we have actually found a really good balance and a very good solution in all points. It is also important for me to stress one thing, because the companies are always talking about: It is difficult for farms to compete internationally with other countries, namely countries which have no animal welfare regulations at all and therefore of course have quite different operating conditions and can produce considerably cheaper. I believe that our task now is to extend the very high standards in the field of animal welfare, to continue to spread them in Europe so that our farmers are not faced with the low-cost competition from other countries and ultimately have to give up the Austrian farms because they have complied with high standards according to our concerns. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] In all cases, I think, we have found very good solutions – solutions that, of course, often seem very hard for businesses, but with which, I think, we have sought future-oriented solutions. At least this was always a very important issue for all of us during the night discussions, for example in the field of laying hens: It is here – and that must also be said – the German-speaking region, even if you look at Switzerland and Germany, far at the top of Europe. There are no such strict rules in any other European country as regards laying hens, as in the German-speaking region, and we in Austria have joined this. We have also announced that we are prohibiting the construction of conventional cages from now on and that there will be a transitional period until the end of 2008 for the conventional cages already in existence, which is three years earlier than in the EU. We have – and this was the second big step – also known to replace the so-called shaped cages, also to ban their new construction immediately and to grant a transitional period of 15 years from the start of commissioning to those farms which have already designed cages. However, we also have – and I attach special importance to it, because I believe that this is important, and because it may, I hope, be the solution for farms, because we want to continue to have Austrian eggs and Austrian laying hens – a system that provides for the certification of new farming systems to be scientifically verified. If they survive scientifically, then they can be placed on the market, then they will be used by the farms, by the farmers and then we will continue, I think, to achieve this difficult spagate, that we have at least half the happy chickens, but at the same time also healthy Austrian eggs for our consumers. [[In the case of the ÖVP and the Freeman, as well as the Abg. May. Sima.]] I know that's not the easiest way, but it's a way of legal certainty for businesses. And that was particularly important to us when we thought about this certification system: that in the future, those farms that adhere to these certified stables and also integrate them in their own farm will have legal certainty that they will carry out animal husbandry in their farms in the next few years exactly with these stableing systems. This is important for businesses, and I hope and am also convinced that this will also lead to a very good end. What was the importance of the ÖVP? – I’ve already responded, it wasn’t that easy at the beginning. It was important for us to draw on experts, and we have in this question – I have already mentioned the names: Professor Troxler, Mag. Gsandtner – brought practitioners and scientists who can tell us what to do. It has always been blamed for the fact that the animal welfare laws that have already existed – there have been ten animal welfare laws in Austria, and we have therefore not found ourselves in the right-wing area at all, but this will always be forgotten in the discussion – that these ten national laws could not be implemented accordingly. That is why we have been looking for people who are looking for practitioners who are just going to help us achieve this, and that is a performance that enables Austrian agriculture to continue to meet its high standards. Why was it still difficult? – Another difficulty was that we found very different legal situations in the countries. As an example, I take the bonding that was strongly debated: For example, the continued linkage was quite different, because there were, of course, exceptional regional provisions in the individual countries. For example, when comparing the federal states, it is noted that some have a lot of farms, such as Lower Austria, Upper Austria and Styria: These have between 24 000 and 28 000 businesses – Vienna, on the other hand, has 16. You can see how different the national laws have logically met these needs. In the case of cattle farming, for example, Upper Austria had around 22,000 cattle holdings in 2002 – which are approximately figures – and Vienna had eight. Therefore, there were very different regulations, because farms were treated differently. We have also tried to find a very good, just solution when it comes to bonding. I think that was the big challenge of this federal law, and I am also convinced that we have succeeded. We have also taken on and implemented many of the demands of the animal welfare People’s desire, such as the Animal Procurement, which is referred to in this Animal Protection Act as the Tierombuds team, and which will not only have one, but will be the Ombudsman in each of the federal states where the implementation of this Animal Protection Act will be. This animal ombudsman will have to work, will do so and will be equipped with the necessary instruments, namely: He is wise and has a party position as well. Ladies and gentlemen! We have not made it easy in the negotiations, and my group agrees with this law. We now have a uniform basic consensus on animal welfare throughout Austria, and I believe that this is very important and is also very important. We must pay great attention to this, because from now on we can build on this basic consensus without constantly blaming each other in newspapers or in discussions, who is worse in animal welfare matters and who is better. I hope and wish that this discussion belongs to the past. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We are doing legal work here and we have finished it today, if you agree. But this does not end work as a whole, because we now have to ensure the implementation of this federal law, and we now have to give appropriate instructions to the companies which are partly very uncertain and also help them. [[President Dr. Khol gives the bell sign.]] I come to the conclusion: The SPÖs, the Greens and the FPÖs have always made it clear to us in the many negotiations that businesses are important to them. We'll take you by the word. Thank you.

There will then be a motion for a resolution later, confirming this. The law provides that, as far as the budget allows, there will be support measures – whether for animal-friendly attitudes or in education or in many other areas. We think that this is a good signal to the outside, because animal protection can not only work on the basis of legal regulations, but animal welfare is a sensation. Animal welfare is something you might need to learn early. With this law, we have also taken the right course in this regard. We now no longer only have many national laws – where there has always been a struggle with the individual countries – but we have a federal law with which Parliament will deal every two years. This is positive, and this has always been a demand of the liberals, since such a large law does not always work in all areas and Parliament naturally has to deal with it in order to make improvements. On the other hand, at the national level, we have the Ombudsman's reporting obligation, which is free of any discretion, to which the authority is obliged to provide assistance and at the same time to provide access to documents, the party's position is for the animal which cannot speak. In view of this, especially this reporting obligation to the country, it is already being worked on at this level where the law is practiced, that improvements may take place immediately, but at least quickly, where they are necessary. Although we have explained the important issue of cage farming not to the farmers concerned but to the consumer in a satisfactory way. Animal torture is animal torture, and we will help these farmers – I hope there will be a four-party consensus – to provide exit aid, transfer aid, so that this will not be implemented on the back of cage farming. They kept cages because it was permitted in the law. We will not punish them, but will help them to implement what we have justly laid down in the law, namely, a ban on cagery. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] We have in pet farming, we have in dog farming, we have drawn a line in many areas along which animal protection is recorded: There is a ban on barbed collars, a ban on these electrical appliances. And it is not only that, but also the purchase of these things is forbidden, the trade with them is forbidden and their use is also prohibited. This will ensure that this law will also make progress in this respect so that it will no longer be used on a daily basis in the future. I'm grateful for being able to do this. On the various areas of bonding. I have to tell you, it was a difficult fight. Farmers who have used the bonding for generations and whose animals have been very good in many areas have not understood that the permanent bonding should be prohibited. You didn't understand. But I would like to say to these farmers: Whoever has managed his business properly so far is only affected by this law, because we want to provide conversion aid, we want to help those who have the opportunity to give animals a runoff. However, we do not want to punish our farmers who have always worked well with the bonding, so we have also found transitional provisions. It should be made clear to the farmers that we have not taken action to destroy them, but to promote them and, in some cases, to develop something positive from them. I would like to mention two areas which were important to us; first, to the objective of the state. Everyone knows that the liberals have always said that the state target is an essential point. It is a wormhead drop that we cannot decide today immediately, but at least the liberals have achieved by their initiative that all four parties jointly submit a motion for a resolution concerning a state targeting animal welfare in § 1. All have recognised that it is important to see and recognize the animal as a suffering being and that it is therefore of particular importance to the protection of animals. This application is as follows: [[Quoted print version removed]] I hope that this federal government will not only work for this, but also implement it! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] On the ritual slaughter: It wasn't far enough for me to have an anaesthetic immediately, i.e. a fraction of a second after that. Our proposal was to accompany scientifically whether the animal still suffers from pain, and also to provide for the possibility of responding immediately and, under certain circumstances, anchoring into regulations if improvements are possible. That is why we are making a motion for a resolution with all the other parties concerning the animal-friendly taking of ritual slaughters. Resolution “The Federal Minister for Health and Women is asked to examine the practice of ritual slaughters, which has been standardised under the strict requirements of the Federal Animal Protection Act, in the light of the progressive scientific findings and, where appropriate, to include new findings that result in an increased level of animal justice in the relevant Regulation.” In my opinion, this is also something you have to acknowledge. First of all, we liberals have acted alone against the ban on shafts, but the other political groups have understood our concerns very well, and they have not blocked us, but have tried to work actively with us to reduce this animal suffering so far that it is no longer present, I hope. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] In many areas we have fought with tradition. We discussed intensively. Hot fire – yes or no? How can we break this tradition – now I say so? We did it with the horses. There is only a five-year transitional arrangement for horses in the bonding, after which it is completely forbidden. You have to be proud of that too. I saw this more critically before, I thought of the carriages in Vienna. I did not know that in Vienna this law is almost before execution. That's why it was easy for me to join this. We have 16 horses ourselves, and I know what bonding means. We did it with the goats, too. The five-year transitional scheme provides enough time for each individual to change. The central requirement of popular desire was to achieve a uniform national animal welfare law instead of a fragmented country legislation. – That's what happened. The second requirement was a clear commitment to the idea and financial promotion of animal welfare in Austria. – This also fulfils the law with its initial paragraph, where federal, state and local authorities commit themselves in a must-set to support animal welfare ideaally and financially. In particular, it has been important to address animal welfare at least a little more visionary and a little more forward-looking in central areas. Therefore, we prohibit, for example, the use, acquisition and possession of electric shock equipment and other force majeure measures in dog training or the sale of dogs and cats in animal traffickings, where no species-friendly and animal-friendly attitude is guaranteed. I should like to give an example of where it has just succeeded in prohibiting the greatest cruelty. The castration of piglets is now carried out throughout Austria without anaesthetics. We know, however, that the nervous system of a young animal, a piggy of a few days, is just as painful, if not even more painful than that of adult animals. ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e9360	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! Mr Keck, on behalf of my colleague Mr Rädler, I should like to tell you that he does not make any excuses from you, but ask you to prove that you are handing over €70 to an animal welfare association. [[I should like to say a few words on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party and the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats.]] The provisions of the Federal Animal Protection Act on Animal Health apply to all animals in Austria, regardless of whether they live at the farm, in the zoo, in the animal trade or in private households. The Federal Animal Protection Act incorporates all animal owners into the legal responsibility for the welfare of the animals. The position of the animals will be subject to detailed regulations in accordance with the provisions of the law. [[President Dr. Khol will again take over the presidency.]] Because many violations of animal welfare The result of lack of knowledge, the Federal Animal Protection Act places great importance on training and information. In order to enable the animal owners to exercise their responsibility, animal acts are obliged to inform their customers of the animal-friendly attitude of the purchased animals. I think every one of us who has pets has already experienced this when he has bought animals for himself and his children. The Federal Government, countries and communities are obliged by the Federal Animal Protection Act to awaken and deepen the understanding of the public, especially the youth, for the idea of animal welfare. This will address the issue of promoting animal welfare throughout Austria. The situation of animal welfare in Austria will be transparent and comprehensible in future because there are reports from the federal states, which will then be dealt with in a Council of Animal Protection in order to propose further possibilities for improvement at expert level. The Federal Animal Protection Act lays down clear provisions with regard to animal torture, the killing of animals and the intervention of animals. It is prohibited to add undue pain, suffering or damage to an animal or to put it into severe fear, such as by unilateral selection of breeding, use of stalk collars, electrical and chemical dressage equipment, by animal fights, dog racing on asphalt and hard soil, irritants or doping agents or the suspension of pets. The suspension of pets always takes place very seasonally. Unfortunately, this abuse occurs very often before the winter and summer holidays. In order to counter this suspension, there will also be rules on electronic labelling, i.e. for the chipping of dogs and cats. I think that this is a very important step in ensuring that, especially in the private sector, which I have very much restricted myself in my remarks, in pet farming, animal welfare is guaranteed. Animal welfare is indivisible! Thank you.

That is to say, over the next five years, for 15,000 horses, we have to make a change from keeping on the hook to holding a box. A small farmer, who has only one horse, may currently have a space requirement of about four square meters; he will have to change to ten square meters in the future. He will probably have to decide to remove a second animal alternatively from the stable or send the horse out of the stable. And at this point a call to politics – and here also to my colleagues –: We do not always appeal to consumers! In this case, the appeal to consumers does not help us at all because the consumer cannot influence the changeover within the courts due to a higher product price thereafter. There are also many horses in the federal army, there are also very many in the state; even there, 90 percent of the bonding is superior. And there, too, the federal government will be required to make such investments and to switch them from the bonding to a boxing position. I ask – and this is really my appeal! – not to change, to which one sends the horses out of the stable, but by making the necessary investments. I believe that there will be a change on the basis of this law for us, but in the long term it will also have a positive impact on horse husbandry in Austria. Millions of animals are transported every year across Europe, partly in days' journeys and often under unimaginable conditions. Animal welfare remains on track for a variety of these transports. What some animals are meant for transport is often limited to animal torture. Unfortunately, all of us have seen the terrible images of overloaded transporters, of abused, half-dried animals that suffer unbearable strains on the transports across Europe. No agreement was reached at the meeting of EU Agriculture Ministers at the end of April 2004. As German Minister of Agriculture Künast recently reported in the German Bundestag, there is little hope that the Irish Presidency will continue to pursue this issue by June of this year, and the Netherlands, for the second half of 2004, in which they hold the Presidency, have already signalled that they do not want to take up the issue. Not compassion, but justice is due to the animals.” My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! It is therefore particularly important to improve national control systems on animal transport. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Our systems are currently only attacking samples, and most of the transports through Austria are unfortunately unlit. Austria has received the urgent recommendation in 2002 by the SANCO control team to rethink possibilities for improving the system of animal welfare inspection during transport. Our proposal, which we are submitting today and which we are dealing with at first reading, aims at improving control. The first step is to submit an annual transport report to the National Council, which makes concrete statements on the total volume of animal transport, the number of checks, the number and causes of the complaints. ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e11644	Mr President. Mr. Minister! The Corporate Law Change Act 2004 includes a wealth of laws that are being changed. But in general, this proposal is about the implementation of an EU directive on the European Company, the so-called Societas Europaea. We also make a deregulation in Austrian share law and thus create “kleine AG” so that this legal form of the stock company also becomes more attractive for family companies or smaller medium-sized companies with a smaller shareholder group. First to the SE, to the Societas Europaea. For multinational companies, the European Company will benefit from the fact that subsidiaries are subject to uniform legislation and not to the rules of the respective State of residence. It is also important to ensure that the European Union is able to work In this way we improve the framework conditions for Austria's economic location and facilitate business settlements by foreign investors. This facilitates cross-border mergers, the relocation of subsidiaries and group holdings and other restructuring and co-operation measures across Europe. According to the directive, the SE can be led to a dualistic system, which is the system we know, as well as – new! – according to the monistic system that is somewhat foreign to our legal form. In the dualist system, there is a clear separation between the Executive Board and the Supervisory Board, which takes control. To a large extent, it also follows our current share law, minority and creditor protection in this new form. The protection of minorities and creditors, of course, also applies to the monistic system. This is where management and control are in the Board of Directors. This constellation is a bit strange to us, but it has been working in Anglo-American space for decades. Therefore, it is not for us to judge whether this is useful or not. I think it makes sense if something has worked somewhere in the long term, to make it possible for us too. It must be borne in mind, however, that the Monistic system in the Anglo-American region is very influenced by a particular understanding of transparency and publicity. We in Austria are publicity stuff, we don't like it so much when numbers are to be published. Although we here in the House provide for permanent publicity obligations, not all businesses really adhere to it. However, when control and management are summarized in a body, it is necessary that these decisions be made transparent. That is why we have, of course, also standardised special disclosure rules in this new law, especially in the fulfilment of the founding conditions. I believe that we are doing a good job in this way and improve the conditions for the Austrian site. The “kleine AG” has been called for for a long time. We did not opt for a law of our own, but made deregulations in the applicable share law in order to comply with this desire for a “small AG”. This is about the fact that family companies or companies with a manageable shareholder group can not only be managed in the form of GesmbH, but also in the form of the AG. The concrete deregulation steps are that we allow a one-person foundation, as we have known at the GesmbH since 1996, that we do not take the founding examination if founders and board of directors or Supervisory Board are a person, that, for example, we give up on the publication in the “Wiener Zeitung” at meetings, and if name shares show who the shareholders are, a registered letter to these shareholders is sufficient. We improve the posting rights in the Supervisory Board for Nuclear Shareholders, it can now nominate up to half of the Supervisory Boards. We improve the use of electronic media, for example for the Annual General Meeting. You know that: Videoconferencing et cetera; all these electronic media will be able to be used in the future. And: We also make the “Knowledged Media”, which has always been only the “Wiener Zeitung”, electronically possible. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! On Agenda item 4, which will be discussed at the same time, including the Remote Financial Services Act and amendments to the Consumer Protection Act, Mr Ikrath will give an even more detailed opinion. I believe that with these, I say, economic laws in the field of justice have improved the economic environment a little further.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e11796	The next speaker was Mrs Dr. Partik-Pablé. How many minutes can I set the clock? - 5 minutes, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e11815	Ladies and gentlemen! Economic life has become very international, indeed global, world-wide, but precisely our company law regulates the activity only in the national area. There is no supranational form of society that would allow a uniform law to operate in different countries, in accordance with uniform standards. For example, Mrs. Fekter has already done this – today a company with foreign subsidiaries has to deal with national rights again and again when it comes to disspositions in its subsidiaries. This means, of course, a certain difficulty, which increases the cost of reducing transparency, and this has led the EU to draw up a directive to facilitate or facilitate the operation of supranational societies. Important actions of companies should be able to be regulated in such a way that they can be used in accordance with a uniform legal principle, such as the relocation of stock companies, the establishment of stock companies, the conversion of stock companies when they are cross-border. Because of this new legal possibility, I know no matter where I am, how I have to act, namely, according to the law of the company's seat. In all cases, this is a step forward and – we would have had to accept this directive – it would have been of benefit to Austria's economic location. If some complain that there is still a need for a notary awarding or at all the enlistment of a notary, then I would like to point out that the presence of the notary as a great-grand person has many advantages. For small stock companies in particular, it is clear that there is a constant need for changes because it is claimed that there were differences of opinion at general meetings and so on. In order to prevent all this, i.e. abuses, it is in all cases necessary or useful that the notary participate. The notarial protocol also contributes significantly to the fact that there is a safe evidence if there are judicial disputes later. The cost argument is actually not really striking, because the fee for an annual ordinary meeting is around €500 plus sales tax, I've been told – really an amount that can be expected of a stock company! However, this short-term cost burden is largely offset by the greater legal certainty that exists. In fact, I do not want to go further into this law, because Mr Fekter and Mr Jarolim have already explained what it is all about. This provision leads to an improvement in the economic location, but I have already said that. While this law is aimed at the entrepreneur, the second issue that we are still dealing with is something that will give consumers greater protection. It is also about the implementation of an EU directive. Consumer protection will be improved by improved protection for financial transactions that are handled via the Internet or also remotely: more information obligations, more information obligations, and improved withdrawal transactions. For many people who act only by telephone or e-mail, it may be a great temptation to conclude a contract, which they will not even know again later, what they have done there, and which they may not want to fulfil at all. In conclusion: Both of these are laws that we need to implement, because they are EU decisions, but they are safe for the whole economy.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e11948	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I should like to add a few points which concern the European society, which is introduced by this change in company law. It has already been mentioned by a number of previous speakers: On the one hand, it is about a harmonization, namely at European level, of the possibility of companies operating for Europe as a whole under the same company law. This – and this is also positive for the European Economic Area – will also lead to a uniformisation of social legislation in the individual Member States, because it will then have the opportunity to choose their registered office in accordance with that company law and to establish a European society where the best rules exist for them. Cheap – this is not a negative thing, but it promotes the economy, which promotes the opportunities of companies to enter the market, and at the lowest cost to be competitive and create jobs. However, in this law on company law, we have also implemented and incorporated rules for simplifying the company. It is mainly about making it attractive for our SMEs, for our small and medium-sized enterprises, to make the stock company possible, especially if they are not listed. Many rules on the protection of investors investing in companies through the stock exchange are very important for companies that are not investment in stock exchanges, where there may also be only a small number of shareholders, in order to make a stock company really attractive. Why is it important to make this attractive, ladies and gentlemen? It is important, therefore, that the transfer of capital should also be made more possible for small and medium-sized enterprises – and we know the international investigations. In this respect, we are internationally, for various historical reasons, not so good. Corporate equity is simply important to be crisis-resistant in order to be able to pass through difficult times. This should be implemented, though – and this has also been discussed in the Committee; I believe that this is also the view of the Minister or the Minister for Foreign Affairs and Security. to provide for additional arrangements for these “small stock companies” – under quotation marks – and to be implemented in the future. Another step is the introduction of a possibility as already exists in the Anglo-Saxon legal system and in various countries: as an alternative to the current Austrian system with the division between the Supervisory Board and the Executive Board in the future to choose the monist system. This means a single governing body of this stock company, this European company, and in this single governing body there are members who are responsible for the management and those who are responsible for supervision, but not two different bodies. Our previous system will continue to be possible, but alternatively there will also be this so-called monistic system. I would like to say that I am pleased that today the opposition parties will also agree with this law, as Mrs Moser has called it, but in the past, in recent months and years, it would have often given the opportunity to give the consent of economic-enhancing laws. I can only mention the recently adopted tax reform – an important point for Austria's economic location! – various regulations with which we have simplified and unified the funding landscape. On all these occasions, we would have liked to see your consent in the interests of Austrian companies, companies and their employees, but you will still have enough opportunity to agree to this kind of law in the future. – Thank you very much.

May. Molterer: ( towards SPÖ) The true face is now coming out of you!]] [[Abg. Dr. Gusenbauer: You need to clap at Scheibner's speech!]] High house! As you know, there was no official round of negotiations at the Intergovernmental Conference between the failed summit last December and two Foreign Ministers' meetings in May, but only bilateral talks between certain Member States. A further meeting of the Foreign Ministers will be held on 14 May. In June, just three days before the European Council, where the Intergovernmental Conference can hopefully be concluded. I would like to report to you on the state of play on these issues in a row – and start with non-institutional issues. Some of these topics are very important from an Austrian point of view, such as the inclusion of the Charter of Fundamental Rights, the abolition of the pillar structure, the creation of a uniform legal personality, the clearer division of competences between the European Union and the Member States, the respect of national identity, the increased control of the principle of subsidiarity and proportionality and the new role of national parliaments. We, ladies and gentlemen, have achieved a strengthening of the social dimension. There will be a horizontal social clause which states that the Union must respect a high level of employment and social security in all policy areas. We have achieved that the so-called general provision of services, i.e. public transport, waste disposal and health services, remains within the competence of the Member States. As regards water resources, spatial planning and land use, we have made it clear from the outset that we are not prepared to give up unanimity. And that was understood. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] It is also confirmed that animal welfare must be included in the various Union policies. The Union's ability to act in the areas of border controls and asylum immigration is enhanced. However, it remains for Member States to regulate the quantitative access of third-country nationals to the labour market. As far as European security and defence policy is concerned, Austria has achieved exactly what it wanted. In the highly debated aid clause, the Constitution is expressly referred to the specific nature of the security policy of certain Member States, and in the so-called solidarity clause, which applies to acts of terrorism and natural disasters, it is explicitly stated that each Member State itself chooses the means of exercising solidarity, ladies and gentlemen. As regards the scope of the qualified majority provisions, the transition to qualified majority voting is particularly controversial in four areas: first in the field of foreign policy, second in the field of taxation, third in the field of social security and fourth in the field of judicial cooperation in criminal matters. This discussion has not yet been completed. A solution will probably only be available in the last round or even in the last round. Austria stands for qualified majority voting, apart from certain exceptions, because we consider this in a Union of 25 to be also a question of ability to act, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Judicial cooperation in criminal matters is very controversial. You know our position on this: This is one of the few questions we ask for unanimity. Another question concerns the area of water resources. The Presidency has taken account of this, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Now to the institutional package: As you know, the big institutional questions remain open. The demands of each Member State are on the table. It is now up to the Presidency to consider a balanced compromise package. The Federal Government is very well prepared for this final round. We have established an alliance of equally small and medium-sized Member States, which have defined some common principles and, above all, want to work together in this final phase of the negotiations. All these states share our position as set out in the principle paper. They reject the non-voting Commissioners proposed by the Convention, who are now quite out of the table, and insist that the definition of the double majority of a parity of quotas for the state and for the population criterion is as close as possible. I'm sure this alliance will prove itself during the remaining weeks. Last but not least to the Middle East Theme, my dear friends Ladies and gentlemen: A solution to the Middle East problem and general tensions in this area, but also between the Arab world and the West, are a priority concern of the European Union's common foreign and security policy. Austria brings into this important Includes his good relations with the countries of the region and his traditional experience and contacts with this area, which has always been at the heart of Austrian foreign policy interest. A uniform position in Europe is supported by Austria in a sustainable manner, as is the case with decisions taken by the National Security Council in connection with the Iraq question, ladies and gentlemen. Austria also supports the efforts of the UN Security Council, which is currently preparing a new resolution on Iraq to enable the transfer of sovereignty to an Iraqi transitional government from 30 June of this year. In any event, this article is intended to ensure that the operations like those in 2000 with regard to Austria – we were the sufferers at the time – can no longer be repeated. Question 3: Austria has always been committed to strengthening the common foreign and security policy of the European Union. Institutionally, the draft Constitution provides for the creation of a European Foreign Minister/A foreign minister who, under a double-hut, unites the former functions of the Commissioner for Foreign Affairs and the High Representative for Foreign Policy. Question 4: I have also referred to this question in my introduction. Question 5: Austria has always sought a dialogue with this space so important to us and has been supporting the dialogue of religions and civilisations for many years. The membership of Austria also plays a major role in the network for human security and the commitment of the foreign minister in this regard. We do not want to push external concepts into the Arab and Islamic world, but rather to cooperate in reform measures in a partnership. As a concrete Austrian contribution, I also see the recent visit of two members of the Iraqi Government Council in Austria, with which, among other things, the President of the Federal Republic of Germany, but also in my capacity as Minister of Economic Affairs, I was able to have detailed discussions on the future economic cooperation between Austria and Iraq. Iraq has always taken a privileged position as an economic partner in Austria, and it is useful to try to raise these economic relations back to the traditional level in the future. On question 6: The European Union and its Member States, with Austria actively participating from the outset, have made a central contribution, through their reliable political, humanitarian and economic commitment, to preserving the main public and private Community structures in the Palestinian territories. At the political level, the European Union fulfils this requirement by its central role in the preparation of the Road Map in the international Middle East quartet. Without the European Union, there would be no "road map" and there would be no quartet, ladies and gentlemen. The latest statement of the quartet has also restored confidence in the Arab-Palestinian perception regarding the implementation of the “Road Map”. The European Union is by far the most important donor of humanitarian, social and economic reconstruction programmes. Austria's efforts have always focused on the development and promotion of social services, particularly in the field of health, but also on vocational training in the public and non-governmental sectors. Since the outbreak of the second Intifada more than three years ago, we have increased the particularly difficult situation of the refugee population. To answer question 7: At the latest, the Madrid attack has led us in a terrible and terrible way that the European Union must make greater efforts in the fight against terrorism. The EU Heads of State or Government's Declaration of 26 March 1994. This year, there are significant innovations to this. With the Dutch de Vries, the newly created office of an EU anti-terrorism coordinator was occupied. Austria supports the creation of this position as well as the re-establishing Intelligence Capacity, a unit in the Council Secretariat, which aims to enable an improved analysis of threats and a faster response. Question 8: As early as 1963, the then European Economic Community had concluded an Association Agreement with Turkey, which contained a prospect of accession. According to the Helsinki European Council in 1999, negotiations will not begin until Turkey meets the political criteria formulated by the Copenhagen European Council in June 1993, namely institutional stability as a guarantee of democratic and rule of law, human rights and respect and protection of minorities. The Copenhagen European Council decided in December 2004 to decide on the opening of accession negotiations with Turkey, provided that the political criteria are met. Although the final progress report of the European Commission of autumn 2003 and the conclusions of the Brussels Council of December 2003 refer to ‘effective reform steps’ of the Turkish Government, it shows that the political criteria are not fully met. Although the fulfilment of the economic criteria is not a prerequisite for the opening of accession negotiations, Austria has repeatedly called for the possibility of Turkey being able to meet these criteria even before accession negotiations are opened. Austria has also committed itself as the first EU Member State to making an evaluation of the cost of any membership before taking a decision on the opening of accession negotiations, and the European Commission has promised this, ladies and gentlemen, to do so. Finally, Austria has repeatedly called for the European Union's willingness to join Turkey. Any further accessions must be compatible with a dynamic continuation of European integration. On the question 9: In my introduction, as I believe, I have also given sufficient views on this. Question 10: Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! In particular, for the small euro area countries, the draft Constitution has put in place decisive improvements in the legal framework for the Stability and Growth Pact. I would like to take the two of them, both of them, to ensure that the Stability and Growth Pact will be respected by all members in the same way in the future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] First improvement: In the Stability and Growth Pact, the Commission is given a right to propose the existence of an excessive deficit. At present, the Commission can only make recommendations. However, a Commission proposal, as you know, would have to be amended unanimously, in contrast to the recommendation, by the Member States. The Council will no longer be able to adopt a recommendation by majority voting and will prevent the establishment of an excessive deficit. Second improvement: In the recent round of the Intergovernmental Conference, Austria has achieved that price stability is enshrined in the objectives of the Union. . Mr Ollinger, we discussed this yesterday. We wanted this, and we did it. Price stability is a high value! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] On the question 11: The current level of expenditure of just under 1% of EU gross national income is given; You know that. According to Austria and some other non-essential Member States, all the priorities of the enlarged Union can well be achieved within this framework of 1 percent of gross national income. Funding for new Member States is also sufficiently available within this 1 per cent if the budget is reworked accordingly. Austria argues that the weight of structural and cohesion policy lies in supporting the new Member States. We cannot accept the Commission's proposal after an increase in expenditure by almost a third, ladies and gentlemen, This is neither understandable to our citizens nor to us if the European Union is exempted from the consolidation process, which is also subject to national budgets – we know that. Question 12: In particular, within the framework of the Intergovernmental Conference: There is also close cooperation on issues of the Western Balkans, the Western Balkans and the so-called security partnership in the field of internal security. Last to question 13: This question is primarily addressed to the Federal Minister for the Interior Dr. Strasser, but from my point of view, I would like to say in general: The Dublin Convention has been applicable since 1. May, of course, also for the new EU Member States. People coming to Austria via these countries can therefore no longer be included in the Austrian asylum procedure. It is therefore expected that the number of asylum seekers will fall. Once more I would like to ask you to understand that I have answered this urgent question, as I am in favour of Mrs Foreign Minister, thank you, Mr President, for granting the word.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e17753	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! 2004 is not only the year of election to the EU Parliament, but 2004 is also the European Year of Education through Sport. The EU has repeatedly stressed the important social role of sport, in particular its educational function. In 2000, there was a statement in Nice that sport not only has educational, but also cultural functions, which are to be taken into account to a greater extent. Sports also promotes skills that are counterweight to various developments, such as technological developments that may bring a lack of movement or sometimes isolation and boredom. Sport can be integrated, but it also has wider objectives, because I believe it is also a key instrument for combating discrimination and is really an optimal means of combating social exclusion, because sport really can involve all levels of society. Disabled people are integrated and equal opportunities between men and women are also promoted. Today is the “European Year of Education through Sport”, and the various activities carried out this year are coordinated by the EU Commission. A large number of projects are available and are coordinated and co-financed from a Community budget. As far as I know, €5.6 million is available for the various projects. The Austrian projects are worth €220 000. We have now set up a national network, created a financing stop, the "Open 2004", and regional, local, national and international projects are also being supported. In addition, there are €150 000 available for the various projects. The various projects run through an action plan that includes and supports various events, which finance various projects, organise competitions, also organize technical events with the university, with schools and with various academies, and also prepare studies on sport and education through sport. We have several competitions, such as a sign competition called “sportlch” where children between the ages of 4 and 10 can make drawings in connection with sports. The closing date is tomorrow. We already have 1 000 entries. There is also an essay competition. We already sorted out the best 80 entries. There's a video competition. 15 of the best videos come into the narrower choice at ORF. You see, the interest is very great. Children and young people have shown a great deal of commitment in all these competitions. There is also a song competition called “songFAIRsuch. ” There everything that has to do with fairness in sports will be tried to present in a musical way. We have also sent the 15 best to the radio station “Ö3” for closer choice. We always celebrate the “Day of Sport” at Heroes Square in September. This year the “day of sport ” is dedicated especially to youth. “Youth at the ball” will call this event. In doing so, the youth has increased the possibility of contact with Austrian athletes, with all associations, and of looking at the whole world of sports. We have a “Young Researcher Seminar” in Innsbruck, where young people deal with sports management, their views of Austrian sport, with their visions for the future. This year, the Austrian Sports Scientific Society is also dedicated to this topic and holds a large congress in November. We have a lot of levels of education for movement and sport in schools across Europe. We have integrated in 15 schools of performance athletes, a total of 1,018 from 47 different sports. In addition, we have started two school experiments in Vienna, where in secondary level 1 performance athletes are already integrated into the school business or where performance sports are integrated into the school business. Of course, we have also put very many priorities outside of school, because education does not necessarily have to be called “education in school”. The “European Year of Education through Sport” is intended to show that sports can be very well educated. The State Secretary for Sport has launched an initiative called “Fit for Austria”. Among them is his already known project “SportKids”, which is more than proven. As far as I know, we are currently running seven pilot projects in kindergartens, where kindergarten children can compete in a poly-sportive training, where they can first learn about the social skills of sports. [[Call at the SPÖ:Who pays all this?]] Yes, the communities see that in sport in the European Year of Education through Sport there is really great potential. In the final effect, it doesn't matter who pays it, essentially, who pays it. [[Abg. [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Worm.]] Secondly, we have made adult adoption more difficult. That's where we want to fight abuse. We rely on the right of the home country of the one to be adopted. If an adult adoption is known and permitted there, it is also possible with us. If this is not allowed in the home country of the one to be adopted, then he cannot come to us and be adopted here. The third point concerns the revision of the oral will. We make this a real emergency emergency, if you are not able to test in writing. But it is temporary and loses its validity. So, if the acute danger or obstacle, which cannot be tested in writing, is over, then in three months you will have to make a written re-examination of the will. We also want to stop abuse that has always occurred. The fourth point: In fulfillment of the Government's proposal, the right of inheritance of nephews and nieces in favour of the surviving spouse is to be limited. That means: In the case of a childless couple, there is already a great consensus in society that in the case of inheritance the position of the surviving partner should be strengthened towards the large family, and we take this into account. In practice, this means: For example, the spouse survives her husband, she receives a third next to the children. If the man does not have children, but siblings and parents – perhaps there are also grandparents – the wife receives two thirds. But if the brothers and parents of the deceased no longer live, then now the nieces and nephews should not get anything, but everything the spouse says, the spouse. I believe that this is justified today. These amendments were partly already contained in the Government Convention. The resort has prepared them perfectly. Thank you for that!

ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-27-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00062_d7e19822	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! One of the documents now under negotiation regulates the legal succession in the matter of origin that now not only the husband, but also the child, is entitled to request the finding that the child does not originate from the husband of the mother. The Austrian system of the right of descent is based on the principle of social origin. If a man recognizes his fatherhood as an unmarried child, he thereby becomes a father. There is no control of the correctness of office. The protection of social families is thus more important than the interest in determining biological origin. Nevertheless, this new template serves, above all, the interests of the child, that the genetic father is actually found. This template now also opens recognition-willing unmarried fathers a trial to obtain security over their possible paternity through a DNA gene analysis. In any case, practice is expected to increase Court of Justice. It is estimated that more than 2,500 such opinions are already being produced in Austria. Interestingly, of course, the question that is often asked – a very serious question of great scope – is: Is that my child or not? because about 10 percent of all children born in a marriage are not from the husband. I would like to thank Mr Böhmdorfer, all his staff and the staff in the Ministry of Justice for this presentation. This also takes into account the demands of practitioners and finds a constitutional, practical and balanced solution. Thank you.

The Council of Europe's Anti-Doping Convention approved by the National Council has led to the establishment of the Austrian Anti-Doping Committee. In 2003, 73 governments signed the Copenhagen Declaration at the World Anti-Doping Conference. The Council of Europe and UNESCO want to draw up this Copenhagen Declaration. In Austria, it is also intended to create its own clear federal legislation, which takes into account international standards. The motion for a resolution provides that the Federal Government, in cooperation with the federal states, intends to develop a government template which provides a constitutional basis for providing for international standards for the organisation and implementation of doping investigations, and also, internationally oriented, sanctions, provides for an appeal and establishes an independent and non-disputed legal remedy. Finally, I can say: International standards ensure equal conditions of competition and thus fairness among athletes. I am pleased that all four parliamentary parties support this motion for a resolution and hope for rapid implementation.
